14:I[8669,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DateSidebar"]
15:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
16:T9f8,方法上，PINT 的设计很干净：从一个现成 SSL encoder 出发，利用真实 parallel speech 和伪 parallel augmentation 两类监督，把不同 realization 中共享的内容压出来。论文的关键不是重新发明 codec，而是把“语义目标”这件事单独拿出来优化。

训练数据分成几类。真实 parallel 语料来自 ARCTIC、CHAINS、CSTR-VCTK、EnDialects、ESD、TIMIT 等，覆盖不同说话人、口音、风格和情绪；非 parallel 语料用 LibriSpeech 960h、Tedlium-3 374h 扩展覆盖面，并通过同一语音的随机增强在线构造 pseudo-parallel 对；作者还用 Kokoro 合成额外 parallel 样本。这里的取舍很重要：只靠真实 parallel 数据，覆盖面不够；只靠增强，学到的多半是噪声不变性而不是说话人/韵律不变性，所以两者结合。

训练目标围绕“跨 realization 对齐”展开。虽然给出的全文片段没有完整公式，但从方法描述可以看出它同时约束连续表示和离散 token：一方面让相同 transcript 的不同语音在时间对齐后靠近，另一方面配合 aggressive augmentation，迫使模型把不稳定因素视为 nuisance。作者强调 word-level forced alignment，这说明他们不是做粗粒度句向量对齐，而是在局部时间轴上保留内容结构。这样做比直接蒸馏 ASR 文本更合适，因为文本会丢掉时长、停顿、局部边界等对 codec 和生成模型有用的帧级 grounding。

论文还把“好 token”的定义说得比较完整：不仅要 content capture，还要 nuisance invariance。很多已有方法只优化前者，比如保留足够音素信息；PINT 认为如果后者做不好，token 仍然不是好的 semantic token，因为同内容不同表面形式会映射到不同离散序列。这个定义直接解释了为什么后续可以配合 RLE 和 BPE 获得更高压缩率：重复帧和稳定片段被真正折叠了，而不是把 speaker/prosody 的抖动也编码进去。

与已有工作相比，PINT 和 ContentVec、DC-Spin、StableToken、NAST 有交集，但重点不同。ContentVec/DC-Spin 更偏 speaker invariance；StableToken 更偏噪声鲁棒；很多 codec 工作则默认 HuBERT/WavLM/Whisper 特征已经足够“语义”。PINT 的区别在于，它把 parallel data 作为核心监督信号，直接针对“同内容多实现”这个结构化事实做表示清洗，而且结果是 drop-in 的：可以作为现有 codec 或 speech LM 的 semantic target，而不要求重做整个系统。17:T40c,This matters for pretraining in at least three ways. First, it reframes speech token quality in language-model terms rather than only downstream task terms: lower conditional entropy, better compressibility, and easier autoregressive prediction. That is the right lens if the tokens are meant to feed speech LMs. Second, it argues that many speech codec and speech-generation systems may be bottlenecked not by decoder architecture but by noisy semantic targets upstream. If the target sequence itself is unstable, the LM is forced to model nuisance variation that should never have been there. Third, it offers an alternative to collapsing speech into ASR text. PINT aims for content-only discrete units while preserving frame-level temporal grounding, which is important for codec-style generation and unified audio-language modeling. The contribution is not a narrow probe improvement; the gains line up across disentanglement, discriminability, compression, and LM perplexity, which suggests a real representation-level improvement.18:Ta3c,PINT starts from a pretrained SSL speech encoder and fine-tunes it using parallel utterances plus aggressive augmentations so that different realizations of the same content align in representation space and, ultimately, in discrete token space. The paper’s design choice is notable: instead of redesigning the codec stack, it isolates the semantic-target problem and improves the upstream representation that existing codec or speech-LM systems can consume as a drop-in replacement.

The training data mix is important. True parallel corpora include ARCTIC, CHAINS, CSTR-VCTK, EnDialects, ESD, and TIMIT, covering variation in speaker, accent, style, and emotion. Non-parallel corpora such as LibriSpeech 960h and Tedlium-3 374h are used to broaden linguistic and acoustic coverage; pseudo-parallel pairs are created online by applying stochastic augmentations to the same utterance. The paper also synthesizes additional parallel samples with Kokoro. This combination is a sensible compromise: real parallel data teaches invariance to speaker and style, while augmentation-based pseudo-parallel data expands coverage and robustness.

A key implementation detail is forced alignment at the word level. That suggests PINT is not learning only sentence-level invariance, but preserving local temporal structure while aligning content across realizations. This is one reason the method differs from using ASR text as the semantic target: text removes nuisance factors, but it also removes frame-level grounding that modern speech codecs and generators rely on.

Conceptually, the paper defines a semantic token with a dual criterion: content capture and nuisance invariance. Many prior methods emphasize the first and under-specify the second. PINT argues that without invariance, the token sequence is still not truly semantic, because identical content does not map to a stable discrete form. That framing also explains the compression angle: once nuisance variation is removed, run-length encoding and BPE can collapse repeated frames and stable spans more effectively, yielding lower bitrate and easier AR modeling.

Relative to prior work, PINT overlaps with ContentVec, DC-Spin, StableToken, and NAST, but the emphasis is different. ContentVec and DC-Spin focus more on speaker invariance; StableToken focuses more on noise robustness; many codec papers simply inherit HuBERT, WavLM, Whisper, or w2v-BERT features as semantic targets. PINT’s distinction is to use parallel data as the primary supervision signal for representation cleaning, explicitly exploiting the same-content/multiple-realizations structure.19:T647,The experimental story is convincing because the metrics form a coherent chain rather than a single isolated win. The headline numbers are: speaker probe accuracy drops from 93.1% to 1.2%, a 98.7% relative reduction, indicating that speaker information is largely removed; ABX discriminability improves by 42%, showing that phonetic/content distinctions are preserved or even sharpened rather than collapsed; and on the same 85M decoder-only Transformer, test perplexity on PINT tokens is 1.95 versus 2.78 and 2.67 for HuBERT and WavLM tokens, a 27–30% reduction. The training-dynamics result is especially important: PINT reaches WavLM’s final perplexity in 23× fewer steps, which points to better optimization and sample efficiency, not just a better endpoint.

For pretraining researchers, this is the most relevant part of the paper. Probe results alone would not prove that the tokens are better LM targets. But lower perplexity and faster convergence on a fixed LM architecture strongly suggest that the token sequence has become more predictable in the right way.

The paper also appears careful about evaluation hygiene. Splits are constructed to avoid transcript leakage, including speaker holdout for CSTR-VCTK and transcript-grouped splitting where official partitions do not exist. That matters because parallel-data methods can otherwise overstate invariance by leaking repeated text across train and test. Additional evaluations include emotion probing on RAVDESS and LM training on a 6k-hour clean subset of LibriLight, covering both representation purity and practical modeling utility.1a:T735,Start with the introduction’s formulation around H(z|c). That section gives the cleanest statement of why unstable semantic tokens are a modeling problem rather than just a representation nuisance. Then move to the method section and focus on the data construction: true parallel data, pseudo-parallel augmentation, and synthetic parallel samples. The exact balance among these sources determines what kind of invariance the model can realistically learn.

After that, read the experiments in three passes. First, check the nuisance-removal evidence: speaker and emotion probes, token stability under perturbation, and any entropy or consistency analysis. Second, check the content-preservation evidence: ABX or phonetic discriminability. Third, spend most of the time on the LM results: perplexity tables, convergence curves, and any compression statistics. Those are the results that connect the tokenizer directly to pretraining efficiency.

If there are ablations, prioritize the ones that isolate the effect of real parallel data versus augmentation-only training, continuous-only versus joint continuous/discrete optimization, and backbone choice. Also pay attention to any RLE/BPE compression analysis. That part is easy to overlook, but it is where the paper most clearly links representation invariance to sequence modeling efficiency.

Two questions are worth keeping in mind while reading. First, is PINT learning linguistic content specifically, or merely the residual that stays stable across the available parallel corpora? Second, could stronger invariance remove para-linguistic information that some generation systems still need? The paper argues that content is preserved and modeling improves, but the division of labor between semantic and acoustic streams remains an open design question for expressive speech generation.1b:T42f,On the hardware side, the setup from Part 1 is reused: a 50:50 beam splitter, two photomultiplier tubes (PMTs), and an FPGA that converts single-photon detection events into a bitstream, forming a QRNG. On the software side, the author plans to modify the sampling logic in llama.cpp and vLLM in two ways: first, introduce a probability floor to prevent low-probability tokens from being zeroed out and thus erased in the multiverse sense; second, use Galois field arithmetic to map QRNG bits onto the token probability distribution while compensating for bias. The system is explicitly not a quantum computer — no entanglement, no superposition of token sequences, no parallel quantum computation. It simply uses single-event quantum measurements as a true randomness source, such that under MWI each sample forks into physically real world branches. The LLM's role is to constrain the high-entropy space of uniform quantum randomness into a low-entropy, semantically meaningful distribution, avoiding the almost pure noise that uniform Unicode sampling would produce.1c:T85d,方法分成两部分。第一部分是 on-policy distillation，目标是降低 domain-level variance。作者不是继续用离线 teacher-student 蒸馏，而是让 drafter 在真实请求分布上在线采样，再用 target model 的分布做蒸馏更新。这里的关键设计有两个。其一是 reverse-KL，而不是常见的 forward-KL/CE。reverse-KL 会更强调 mode-seeking，鼓励 drafter 把概率集中到 target 更确信的 token 上，这和 speculative decoding 的 acceptance 目标更一致，因为 acceptance 取决于 p/q 的比值，q 在 target 低概率区域铺得太宽会直接伤害接受率。其二是 entry-wise divergence clipping，用来抑制 diffusion drafter 训练时局部 token 分布偏差过大导致的不稳定更新。论文把这个 clipping 设计成逐项裁剪的散度约束，而不是简单全局梯度裁剪，针对的是在线蒸馏里少数高偏差 token 把整个 batch 拉坏的问题。

第二部分是 adaptive length head，目标是降低 token-level variance 带来的无效验证开销。作者在 drafter 上额外接一个长度预测头，根据当前 prefix 和 draft 置信度动态决定 candidate length，而不是固定 k。直觉上，这相当于把“是否继续多起草几个 token”变成一个条件决策：当后续位置接受概率已经明显下降时，提前截断，少让 target 做无谓并行验证；当当前上下文容易预测时，再把长度放长，吃到单步并行的收益。这个设计和 AR speculative decoding 里固定 k 的思路不同，更接近 token-budget allocation。

系统层面还有两点实现值得注意。一个是 asynchronous training-inference pipeline，把在线蒸馏更新和在线服务解耦，避免 adaptation 直接阻塞请求路径。另一个是 adaptive request scheduling，在 serving engine 里根据请求状态和 drafter 行为做调度，尽量把在线适配带来的额外开销藏在系统流水线里。论文强调高并发下 prior 方法会退化到不如 AR baseline，说明他们不是只做算法指标，而是把 queueing 和 GPU 利用率也纳入了设计约束。1d:T818,The method has two main components. The first is on-policy distillation, designed to reduce domain-level variance. Instead of relying on purely offline teacher-student distillation, the drafter is adapted on requests sampled from the live serving distribution, with the target model providing the supervision signal. Two design choices matter. First, the distillation objective uses reverse KL rather than the more common forward KL or plain cross-entropy. That choice is aligned with speculative decoding: reverse KL is more mode-seeking, so it encourages the drafter to concentrate probability mass on tokens the target strongly prefers, which is exactly what improves acceptance under the p/q ratio. A drafter that spreads mass too broadly over low-target-probability tokens hurts acceptance. Second, the paper introduces entry-wise divergence clipping to stabilize online updates. This is more targeted than generic gradient clipping: it limits the effect of a small number of badly mismatched token entries that would otherwise dominate the update and destabilize adaptation.

The second component is an adaptive length head, aimed at token-level variance. Instead of using a fixed candidate length k, the drafter predicts how long the draft should be for the current prefix and confidence pattern. The idea is simple but important: if acceptance probability is likely to decay quickly over the drafted span, stop early and avoid wasting target-side verification on unlikely tokens; if the context is easy, draft longer and exploit the one-pass parallelism. This turns candidate length into a conditional compute-allocation decision rather than a static hyperparameter.

The system layer also matters. The paper adds an asynchronous training-inference pipeline so online adaptation does not block serving, and an adaptive request scheduler inside the serving engine. That is important because many speculative methods look good in isolated latency measurements but degrade under queueing pressure and high concurrency. AdaFlash is explicitly designed for that regime.1e:T6ee,The evaluation spans eight benchmarks and three foundation models, including both dense and MoE targets. That is stronger evidence than a single-model study. The headline number in the abstract is up to 5.3x speedup over standard autoregressive decoding. Against prior speculative baselines, including EAGLE-3, multi-token prediction, and DFlash, the method also reports up to 66% higher throughput at high concurrency.

The most important results are likely not the average speedup alone, but whether the proposed controls actually reduce variance. There should be three result groups worth checking closely. First, acceptance-rate distributions across domains: AdaFlash should narrow the cross-domain spread that diffusion drafters otherwise exhibit. Second, the effect of the adaptive length head on verification cost: the accepted length may drop only slightly, while the number of target-side verified tokens drops more, yielding better throughput. Third, the high-concurrency serving curves: many speculative systems lose their gains once scheduling overhead, verifier contention, and extra kernels become dominant; AdaFlash claims to remain net-positive there.

For ablations, the key question is whether reverse KL and divergence clipping are both necessary. Online adaptation without reverse KL would likely improve alignment only modestly, while removing clipping should make updates less stable, especially on out-of-distribution batches. For the length head, the right comparison is fixed-k versus dynamic-k in terms of accepted tokens per unit of target compute, not just raw acceptance. The abstract does not expose model sizes, adaptation frequency, or online training budget, so those details need to be pulled from the main text to judge deployment realism.1f:T434,Start with the motivation figures that visualize the two variance types. If the evidence for domain-level and token-level variance is weak, the rest of the paper matters less. Then read the method section with a narrow focus: how exactly reverse KL is defined, where the teacher distribution comes from, what quantity is clipped by the entry-wise divergence clipping, and why that objective should correlate better with acceptance than standard CE distillation. After that, inspect the adaptive length head: what target it predicts, how it is trained, and whether it is effectively learning an acceptance profile or directly optimizing a cost-benefit proxy. Finally, read the high-concurrency serving results and the systems section to separate algorithmic gains from engineering gains.

For a skim, 25 to 35 minutes is enough. Read the motivation figures, the method overview, the main comparison table, and the concurrency plots. If you are interested in transferring the idea to other non-AR drafters, spend extra time on the OPD objective and the dynamic-length mechanism.20:T7b3,The main idea is a joint routing framework augmented with a lightweight latency estimator. For each incoming query, the router combines three signals: expected response quality, monetary cost, and predicted latency at a specific model instance. The difficult part is latency estimation. Unlike cost, latency depends on the current state of the serving system. The paper proposes a Serving Framework Simulation (SFS) estimator that simulates the evolution of autoregressive token batches at each instance. It tracks active requests, separates prefill and decode work, and incorporates the batching/admission/scheduling behavior of the underlying serving framework.

This is a more structured approach than using prompt length, expected output length, or queue length alone. In modern continuous batching systems, prefill is typically compute-bound while decode is more memory-bandwidth-bound, and the mix of the two phases strongly affects how much delay a new request will see. The estimator therefore models not just how long a query is, but what kind of workload is already occupying the instance.

The overall routing pipeline appears to have three components: (1) an accuracy/cost predictor from prior query-routing work, (2) the SFS latency predictor conditioned on per-instance workload state and serving policy, and (3) a routing objective that selects the model instance under a latency-aware utility or constraint. A notable design choice is that latency is not treated as a pure black-box regression target. Instead, the estimator uses serving mechanics explicitly, which should make it more robust across different batching policies and heterogeneous deployments.

Relative to prior latency-aware routing work based mainly on output-length heuristics, the key difference is the explicit modeling of prefill/decode composition and current instance workload. That matters especially for long-generation queries, where decode backlog dominates user-visible delay.21:T417,The experimental story has three parts: estimator fidelity, end-to-end routing quality, and robustness under dynamic workloads. The paper reports that the latency-aware router achieves a better accuracy–cost–latency tradeoff than both latency-agnostic query routers and system-only load-balancing baselines such as round-robin or shortest-queue style policies.

The full numeric tables are not visible in the provided text, so I should avoid inventing exact values. Still, the evaluation clearly targets TTFT and related latency metrics under varying workload conditions, likely including different prompt/output length distributions, load levels, and serving policies. The important checks when reading the paper are: whether latency prediction error remains controlled across workload regimes; whether the gains come from genuinely better model-instance selection rather than simply avoiding busy replicas; whether the advantage widens in high-load or long-output settings; and how sensitive the router is to output-length prediction errors.22:T49a,The core idea is a two-level rubric system. The upper level is a structured meta-rubric that captures how a complete answer should be organized. It includes typed components such as simple knowledge, strict lists, flexible lists, and processes, along with importance tiers like answer-critical, valuable, and context, all grounded in cited evidence. The lower level is a mechanically compiled flat checklist of binary items used for grading.

This split is the main contribution. Rich structure is needed to express completeness faithfully, but judges are more reliable on flat pass/fail checks. GAMUT keeps the expressive representation for rubric authoring and verification, while using the compiled binary form for low-variance automatic scoring.

The benchmark targets what the paper calls everyday deep research: realistic, image-grounded, knowledge-seeking questions that require multi-step web research and synthesis. Construction uses frontier search-capable LLMs together with expert human annotators over multiple rounds, with human-verified web evidence. A text-only variant is also released to separate visual identification from completeness of knowledge synthesis.23:T63a,SciHazard contains 2,400 hazardous questions and 600 oversafety questions across 12 disciplines: chemistry, biology, physics, computer science, psychology, sociology, medicine, pharmacology, environmental science, economics, law, and agricultural and food science. The data is built with a dual-track scientific misuse generation pipeline. For seven material domains, the pipeline extracts 1,548 controlled substances from regulatory sources such as CWC, CDC/APHIS Select Agents, DEA schedules, EU REACH, and UN conventions. These substances are treated as hazard primitives. Questions are expanded along the weaponization lifecycle: upstream acquisition and substitution, midstream optimization and scale-up, downstream stabilization and storage, and delivery and dissemination. The pipeline also incorporates technical bottlenecks, such as stability, screening evasion, or transmissibility. For non-material domains such as computer science, psychology, sociology, economics, and law, the benchmark uses a scenario-driven route because the risk comes from exploiting rules, information flows, or systemic logic. The scoring framework, DeHarm-Score, decomposes harmfulness into query hazard severity, refusal behavior, and response-level risk. For non-refused answers, response-level risk is split into Executability and Net-New Risk. Executability is measured with dynamic importance-weighted checklists and critical blocking steps. Net-New Risk uses retrieval-augmented claim extraction and synthesis-barrier verification to estimate whether the response provides new operational capability.24:T5bb,The method is straightforward. The authors build MissingBench-Verified, a 118-image benchmark composed of real photos and carefully selected internet images. Each image is paired with a target element to remove. Most edits are produced with the image editing model Nano Banana 2, and the rest are manually edited with an eraser tool. All edited images are then human-verified to filter out unnatural or failed edits. The evaluation prompt is a three-way multiple-choice question asking whether the queried item is clearly visible, visible but hard to see, or not visible, with structured JSON output including reasoning and final answer.

There are four main evaluation settings. Direct query asks the model to judge visibility on its own. Pre-answer tool injection inserts a simulated perfect detector result before the model answers; the detector always reports the queried item as undetected with low confidence. This tests whether external evidence can steer the model. Post-answer tool injection provides the same evidence after the model has already answered, probing confirmation bias. Thinking with images gives the model access to basic image-processing tools such as cropping, brightness and contrast adjustment, and binarization, to test whether active inspection helps. The paper also varies reasoning effort for GPT-5.2 and includes OWLv2 and LLM-Det as detector-style references, though those comparisons are more sanity checks than direct task matches.25:T6ff,The main result is consistent across models: ten leading VLMs perform poorly on images where essential parts were removed. No model exceeds 75% accuracy on the edited set, and four out of ten fall below 50%. The lowest reported accuracy is 44.1% for Claude Sonnet 4.6. Some models show a strong existence bias. Qwen 3.5 Flash, for example, chooses the 'clearly visible' option in 51.7% of cases, which is even higher than its correct 'not visible' rate of 47.5%.

Tool injection helps only partially. In the pre-answer setting, Kimi K2.5 and Qwen 3.5 Plus and Flash improve by about 14.4% to 16.9%, but overall performance remains modest. The post-answer setting is more revealing because it tests whether models revise an already formed belief. Qwen 3.5 Plus shows the largest gain at 24.5%, but most models improve only slightly, and some even dip a bit, though the authors caution against over-interpreting small changes given the dataset size. Even with a simulated perfect detector, most models still remain below 75% not-visible accuracy; Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview is the only one reported above that threshold, reaching 82.2% in the post-answer setting.

On the original unedited images, models generally identify the queried object or part correctly, which matters because it shows the task itself is not inherently ambiguous. The failure is concentrated in the missing-part condition. The think-with-images setting, tested on only a few models, does not appear to reliably solve the problem. NegAwareQwen also performs poorly, suggesting that narrow finetuning on easier negative cases does not transfer well to this harder visual-negation setup. Because the benchmark has only 118 images, the results are more useful as evidence of a robust failure mode than as a precise leaderboard.26:T509,The system is built on a 15B video diffusion transformer and generates 24-fps video at 540p and 720p. Instead of producing a full long video in one pass, it generates short latent chunks autoregressively. Each chunk is conditioned on camera trajectories and switchable text prompts. The central mechanism is a bounded visual context composed of a global scene anchor, compressed recent history, geometry-aligned spatial memory, and recent-frame conditioning. Temporal memory maintains short-range dynamics and frame continuity. Spatial memory stores past observations and reprojects them into the current target view when the camera revisits a region. This keeps per-chunk compute approximately constant as rollout length grows. Training has three stages: adapt a general bidirectional video prior to world-modeling data; convert it into an autoregressive generator with camera control and spatiotemporal memory; then train with corrupted histories and replay prediction residuals collected from the model’s own rollouts. For inference, the paper uses discrete autoregressive distillation with distribution-matching distillation, self-forcing++, and consistency distillation, reducing sampling from roughly 30 steps to 4 steps per chunk while keeping the control and memory stack active.27:T4a5,The training corpus contains 222,147 clips from seven sources, including internally curated MUGEN and GameVerse data. It mixes real-world videos, gameplay recordings, synthetic renderings, and generated event videos. Each record is normalized into video frames, camera intrinsics and poses when available or recoverable, and timeline-aligned hierarchical captions. Evaluation is performed on iWorld-Bench, covering generation quality, trajectory following, and memory ability. The paper reports stronger overall performance than compared systems. The most informative ablations concern spatial memory, corrupted-history training, replay residuals, and distillation. The reported pattern is consistent: recent-frame conditioning alone is insufficient for revisitation; geometry-aligned memory improves preservation of previously seen regions; training on model-induced imperfect histories reduces long-horizon blur, illumination drift, and geometric drift. The paper also states a clear limitation: the model still represents the world mainly through visible observations, estimated geometry, and visual memory, so object state, physical causality, and long-term task structure remain weak.28:T5ca,Each weight matrix is analyzed in its SVD basis W = U Σ V^⊤, with Σ the singular-value spectrum and (U, V) the output/input singular frames. The central empirical observation is that freely trained RLVR checkpoints stay near-isospectral to their base, and this proximity is not merely a high-dimensional artifact. Three interventions test functional necessity: (1) restoring base Σ while keeping learned U, V in RL checkpoints preserves most acquired gains; (2) training with Σ fixed throughout RL while updating only U and V still achieves strong RL learning; (3) the reverse—freezing frames and updating only Σ—yields only marginal improvement. This leads to spectral inheritance: the base spectrum can be reused, with new behavior carried by both frames. Follow-up ablations show both frames must remain adaptable; remixing only within input/output spans or holding one frame fixed leaves substantially more of the endpoint change unexplained. ISO (Isospectral Optimization) operationalizes this in two complementary forms. ISO-Merger (offline) consolidates multiple specialists trained from a shared base by reusing the shared base spectra and directly combining expert frame changes into a single fixed-spectrum checkpoint, without extra data, rollouts, or distillation. ISO-Optimizer (online) constrains training to the fixed-spectrum manifold of the base weights while allowing both frames to adapt, and can be wrapped around base optimizers such as AdamW or Muon.29:T460,Read carefully, about 1–1.5 hours. Recommended order: (1) Introduction through the definition of spectral inheritance—this is the conceptual core and situates RLVR against pretraining and SFT; (2) the near-isospectrality observation together with the high-dimensional calibration—important for distinguishing geometric proximity from functional reusability; (3) the three key interventions (spectrum restoration, fixed-spectrum training, spectrum-only training) — the strongest evidence for inheritance; (4) ablations on two-frame adaptability, to see why one-sided or span-restricted variants are insufficient; (5) ISO-Merger and ISO-Optimizer sections for how the parameterization is implemented in practice, especially how frame updates are regularized and how the scheme relates to Riemannian/orthogonal constraints. Points worth digging into: whether the same pattern holds for SFT and continual pretraining, whether attention and MLP weights behave differently, how frame-level updates relate to Muon’s orthogonal updates and Apollo’s subspace projections, and whether the story extends to MoE experts.2a:T73b,The method uses a two-level reward design. The model is prompted to produce structured parallel reasoning with explicit tags such as <Parallel>, <Path>, and <Summary>, separating path generation from final aggregation. Training then combines: (1) an outcome reward at the summary/final-answer level, typically based on correctness, and (2) a process reward at the individual-path level.

The core idea is to define the process reward with Shapley values. A set of reasoning paths is treated as a cooperative game, each path is a player, and the utility of a coalition is the quality obtained when a subset of paths is provided to the summarization/evaluation mechanism. Shapley value is then used to measure the average marginal contribution of each path across different coalition contexts. This is a natural fit because the value of a path is not intrinsic; it depends on whether it complements, duplicates, or contradicts other paths.

Exact Shapley computation is exponential in the number of paths, so the paper uses Monte Carlo permutation sampling. For sampled path orderings, it measures the utility gain when a path is added to the current prefix coalition, and averages these gains to estimate the path’s Shapley value. This makes the method tractable.

A Generative Reward Model is used to score the utility of different path subsets, providing the coalition values needed for Shapley estimation. The resulting training signal combines global correctness pressure with fine-grained path attribution. Optimization is built on top of GRPO rather than introducing a new policy optimization algorithm. Relative to prior systems such as Parallel-R1, the main difference is not the existence of parallel reasoning itself, but the explicit decomposition of reward across paths instead of treating the whole path set as one undifferentiated unit.2b:T56f,The experiments are centered on mathematical reasoning benchmarks, with emphasis on Pass@k, especially Pass@16, and on training stability. The headline result reported in the paper is an average 42.3% improvement on Pass@16 over a strong parallel-thinking baseline. That suggests the method is better at converting parallel sampling budget into useful candidate diversity.

From the experimental narrative, the key comparisons include SFT-style parallel reasoning, standard outcome-level RL, and the proposed path-attributed RL. The main takeaways are:
1. Fine-grained attribution beats uniform reward sharing, indicating that credit assignment is a real bottleneck.
2. Training is more stable and converges faster, consistent with the claim that mis-rewarding bad paths injects optimization noise.
3. Monte Carlo Shapley remains useful with a limited number of samples, so the method does not require highly accurate Shapley estimates to help.

The most informative ablations are likely the usual ones: outcome reward only vs process reward only vs both; number of paths; number of Monte Carlo samples; with or without the generative reward model; and robustness when path quality is highly uneven. The excerpt does not expose all table values, but the central empirical claim is clear: the gain comes from better path-level reward allocation rather than from the prompting structure alone.2c:T478,This is a skim paper, not one that requires line-by-line study. Start with the introduction and isolate the central criticism: summary-level reward lets ineffective paths get positive updates. Then read the method section with three questions in mind: how the parallel reasoning structure is organized, how coalition utility is defined and scored by the generative reward model, and how Monte Carlo Shapley is approximated in practice.

In experiments, prioritize the main result table and the training curves. If time is limited, verify three things: whether Pass@k gains are consistent, whether the benefit is really tied to path-level attribution, and whether the evidence for improved stability is convincing.

Two deeper questions are worth keeping in mind. First, whether the Shapley-based reward is aligned with the policy objective or mainly acts as heuristic reward shaping. Second, whether errors in the reward model can amplify attribution noise. Those questions matter more than the headline number if one wants to judge whether this approach can scale to larger reasoning RL settings. A useful read should take about 30-45 minutes.2d:T40d,CASE combines training-time causal alignment with inference-time structural enforcement. During training, it builds counterfactual-CoT, biased-instruction, and empty-instruction data, then applies selective-loss fine-tuning mainly on answer generation. Counterfactual CoTs encourage the answer to change when the reasoning changes. Biased instructions reduce reliance on misleading instruction-level shortcuts. Empty-instruction examples force the model to answer from the CoT when the original instruction is unavailable. At inference time, CASE applies an attention mask so that answer tokens cannot directly attend to instruction tokens; they must access instruction-derived information through the CoT-side hidden states. This avoids the brittle alternative of training a pure p(y|x) model from the start, which can create degenerate rationales or unstable supervision because valid CoTs are not unique. The paper also links the losses to information-theoretic criteria: increasing I(X;Y), increasing I(X;Y|Z), and reducing I(Y;Z|X).2e:T808,The method, S2T-RLHF, uses a two-stage hierarchical credit assignment scheme: sentence level first, token level second. The motivation is simple and well argued. Human preferences are often expressed over semantically coherent units such as claims, instructions, refusals, or stylistic choices, and sentences are a much better proxy for those units than individual tokens. Tokens mostly realize sentence meaning through lexical and syntactic choices. So instead of directly decomposing a sequence-level reward into token-level rewards, the method first allocates reward across sentence-level units and only then performs bounded token-level refinement within each sentence.

A key design choice is that the reward model remains fixed and only sequence-level preference rewards are used. In the first stage, the global reward is distributed over sentence units through a structured cooperative allocation mechanism. The wording suggests that sentences are treated as jointly contributing parts of the response, rather than independently scored fragments. That matters because a response-level preference often depends on interactions across sentences, not just local quality. In the second stage, token-level refinement is explicitly bounded. This is crucial: the paper’s main thesis is that unconstrained fine-grained refinement can inject instability, so token-level signals are allowed only as local adjustments around a sentence-level semantic anchor.

The appendices reportedly provide theoretical discussion around three issues: how sequence-level rewards induce global advantage alignment, how variance amplifies over long horizons, and how gradients scale with response length. The S2T design addresses these by shortening the effective credit-assignment path through sentence-level decomposition, while preserving limited local flexibility through bounded token refinement. Compared with prior dense-reward or token-level credit methods, the novelty is not “more granularity” but “the right granularity first, then controlled refinement.”2f:T4a1,The main empirical claim is that S2T-RLHF improves training stability and robustness across multiple datasets and optimization settings while maintaining competitive preference alignment performance. The provided text does not include the full numeric tables, so exact gains should not be fabricated. Still, the experimental intent is clear: compare standard sequence-level RLHF, finer-grained credit-assignment baselines, and the proposed hierarchical method.

The most important signals to inspect are: whether training curves are smoother; whether reward collapse or severe oscillation is reduced; whether response-length behavior becomes healthier; whether the method remains effective across different optimization setups; and whether the gains come specifically from the sentence-first hierarchy rather than from simply adding denser supervision. The critical ablations should include sentence-level allocation alone, direct token-level refinement, and different bounds on token refinement strength. If those ablations are convincing, they support the paper’s central claim that stability comes from semantic alignment of reward granularity, not from maximizing reward density.30:T5da,The paper introduces a stylized optimization model. Outputs live in a D-dimensional space. The first dimension is the detector-facing attribute, while quality depends on all dimensions. A user chooses two controls: how much work to delegate to the LLM, modeled as a convex combination of human-only output and fully LLM-generated output, and how much post-processing to apply to reduce the detected attribute.

The user maximizes utility, which combines output quality, detector penalty if flagged, production cost, and post-processing cost. The detector itself is modeled as a threshold-based classifier on the first dimension. With this setup, the paper derives conditions under which detector deployment changes optimal user behavior in unintuitive ways.

The contribution is not algorithmic complexity but analytical clarity. The model cleanly separates three quantities: the detected attribute, LLM usage, and output quality. The paper shows that detector deployment has a predictable directional effect on the first quantity, but not on the latter two. In other words, a detector can reduce the feature it sees while still increasing LLM reliance for some users or reducing final quality.

The theory is also connected to the empirically observed rise-then-fall pattern in LLM-associated words and stylistic markers. The interpretation is straightforward: LLM access initially increases the detector-facing attribute, and subsequent detection pressure or social avoidance pushes it back down.31:T4c3,The empirical component mainly serves two purposes: illustrating the theoretical mechanisms and checking whether the predicted qualitative pattern appears in real data. On the theory side, the paper uses figures and parameterized examples to show three effects: detector introduction can increase optimal LLM usage for some users, can reduce output quality even when the targeted attribute is quality-reducing, and can generate a rise-then-fall trajectory for the detected attribute.

These correspond to Theorems 1-4. Theorems 1 and 2 provide sufficient and necessary conditions for increased LLM usage; Theorem 3 gives sufficient conditions for reduced output quality; Theorem 4 shows that the detected attribute weakly increases when users gain LLM access and weakly decreases after detector deployment.

On the empirical side, the paper goes beyond earlier observations based on a few word lists and examines whether rise-then-fall patterns have become more prevalent in the LLM era relative to earlier time windows over the last decade. The answer is yes. This is not a large-scale model benchmark and not a detector-evaluation paper; it is closer to an empirical sanity check supporting the theoretical mechanism.32:T4eb,The analysis uses a minimal fixed-offset retrieval task that isolates pure positional selection, with models trained on short sequences and asked to copy a token a fixed number of positions before the final query position. Building on the existing result that trained attention heads converge to max-margin solutions under gradient descent, the structure of solutions learned with each positional encoding type can be formally derived: 1. RoPE attention logits are proven to be exactly equivariant to relative offset, independent of absolute sequence length. Trained query and key representations develop a dominant direction aligned to the target offset, producing an attention kernel peaked at the correct relative distance that generalizes across lengths. 2. The graceful accuracy decay of RoPE at longer lengths is shown to come from softmax normalization dilution: as context grows, the target token's attention weight is spread across more distractor positions. Linear attention without softmax normalization fits in-distribution data via min-norm interpolation but fails to extrapolate. 3. APE solutions are proven to be pinned to absolute positions within the training length range, so they miss the target entirely when evaluated on longer sequences.33:T51e,The paper introduces the "information shadow" framework with three distinct classes of structural limits. Type I limits arise from the expressivity bottleneck of language itself: structure destroyed when reality is compressed into text cannot be recovered by additional text. Type II limits come from statistical non-identifiability of the training distribution: multiple incompatible generating mechanisms can produce identical text distributions, so no number of samples can distinguish between them. Type III limits are optimization reachability problems: the target function is representable by the architecture but cannot be reached by standard gradient-based training dynamics. For each type, the paper designs a falsifiable probe: Language Compression Residuals for Type I, which compares performance gaps between text-only learners and full-signal learners where the gap does not close with scale; the Counterfactual Distinction Test for Type II, which constructs training distributions consistent with two rules, probes behavior on counterfactual inputs, and validates with small injections of disambiguating data; and Basin Escape Mapping for Type III, which first proves the target is representable, then measures reachability rates under interventions such as initialization, curriculum, and width.34:T458,They define a per-position disagreement random variable δ(x)=log p_M(x|y_<t)−log p_O(x|y_<t), where the oracle is a stronger model from the same family. Taking expectation over V~p_M yields E[δ]=KL(p_M||p_O) as a “gap” term. The second moment decomposes exactly into KL^2 + Var[δ], and Var[δ] is interpreted as risk: how unevenly the oracle reweights the candidate tokens the model still considers plausible.
Mechanism: (1) with scaling, risk concentrates into heavier tails; (2) autoregressive sampling at the first fabrication collapses distributional risk into a realized bias that becomes part of the context; (3) model entropy (felt uncertainty) quickly returns to baseline after onset due to fluent continuation, while oracle-referenced risk persists because the wrong premise is now conditioned on; (4) this creates a “confident but precarious” regime (low entropy, high risk) that bridges consecutive fabrications. They further argue that post-onset risk contraction can causally reduce downstream hallucinations, and that p_M-only detectors are structurally blind to the precarious a","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-21T12:19:38Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2607_19033.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是语音离散语义 来说，这不是小瑕疵，而是直接抬高序列熵、降低可压缩性、增加建模难度。作者的核心判断很明确：如果多个人在不同条件下说的是同一句内容，那么真正跨样本稳定存在的只剩 linguistic content；因此语义 tokenizer 不该继续被动继承 HuBERT/WavLM 的泄漏表征，而应显式利用 parallel utterances 的前端设计，都有直接启发。贡献量级上，这不是简单 probe 改善，而是同时在可分离性、ABX、压缩性和 LM perplexity 上形成一致收益，说明它触到了表示层面的关键约束。","technical_approach":"$16","experiments":"实验最有说服力的地方在于指标链条比较完整，不是只挑一个 probe。论文报告的核心数字包括：说话人 probe 准确率从 更适合生成；但 perplexity 和收敛速度同时改善，说明序列统计结构确实更接近“语言建模友好”的形式。\n\n从文中描述看，作者还评估了压缩性和鲁棒性，并在严格数据切分上避免 transcript leakage：例如 CSTR-VCTK 按 speaker holdout，TIMIT/LibriSpeech 用标准划分，没有官方划分的数据则按 normalized transcript 成组切分。这一点值得注意，因为 parallel 训练很容易在相同文本上发生泄漏，进而夸大 invariance 效果。论文还使用 RAVDESS 做情感 probe、LibriLight 6k clean 子集训练 LM，说明评估覆盖了表示纯度和实际建模两个层面。","reading_guide":"建议先看 Introduction 里对 H(z|c) The same uttered content can map to different token sequences depending on speaker identity, prosody, recording channel, or noise conditions. That raises conditional entropy H(z|c), hurts compressibility, and makes downstream language modeling unnecessarily harder. The paper’s key premise is simple and strong: if many speakers say the same words under varying conditions, the only factor that should remain invariant is linguistic content. To enable controlled experiments, we introduce Moving A","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-21T07:07:19Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2607_18789.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CV"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作关心的问题是：在 text-to-video 预训练中，数据分布、视频复杂度、clip 时长和 caption 质量分别怎样影响模型学习。真实视频数据有太多混杂因素，作者用可控的程序生成数据，把这些因素拆开测。","importance":"对预训练数据研究的启发大于对视频生成模型本身的贡献。它给出一个清晰结论：视频生成的可控性和训练效率很大程度受 caption 质量限制，尤其是 caption precision，即描述是否正确。caption recall，即描述是否完整，也重要，但在预算有限时，正确性优先于完整性。这个结论对大规模多模态预训练的数据管线有直接参考意义，尤其是视频 captioner、clip selection 和数据混合策略。","technical_approach":"作者构建了 Moving Alphabet 测试床：在黑色背景上渲染 1 到 3 个移动字母，控制字体、颜色、大小、位置、方向、速度和视频时长。因为所有视觉属性都来自已知元数据，所以可以生成准确 caption，也可以系统性破坏 caption 的 precision 和 recall。这样可以避免真实视频中语义标注不完整、动作边界不清、caption 主观性强等问题。方法重点不是提出新模型，而是建立可控数据分布，并用细粒度指标评估视觉质量和 prompt adherence。相比直接在真实视频上做统计相关性分析，这种设计牺牲了语义复杂度，但换来更干净的因果观察。","experiments":"实验主要比较三类因素。第一是内容复杂度：只训练单一复杂度的模型不一定在对应复杂度上最好，混合 1-letter、2-letter、3-letter 的均衡数据，能匹配或超过专门训练在单一复杂度上的模型。第二是时长分布：混合 2 秒、4 秒、8 秒视频的训练集也表现更稳，说明预训练阶段不宜过早窄化到单一 duration。第三是 caption 质量：降低 precision 或 recall 都会显著损害性能，其中 precision 损害更大。caption 被污染后，模型可能需要约 2 到 4 倍 compute 才能达到 clean caption 模型的验证损失。后处理补救有限：classifier-free guidance 能改善一部分 prompt following，但不能消除差距；用少量 clean data fine-tuning 只能在中等污染条件下恢复最多约 55% 的 foreground PSNR 损失。","reading_guide":"建议泛读 Introduction 和 Moving Alphabet 数据构造部分，重点看 caption corruption 的定义、precision/recall 如何被操作化、以及评估指标如何对应视觉属性和 prompt adherence。实验部分优先看三组结果：复杂度混合、duration 混合、caption precision/recall 消融。预计 20 到 30 分钟可以读完主要信息。值得深挖的是它的实验范式：用程序生成数据做预训练数据因果分析。这种思路可以迁移到图像、语音、代码数据的 controllable ablation。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies how training data distribution, clip duration, visual complexity, and caption quality affect text-to-video model training. Instead of relying on real video corpora with many confounders, it builds a controlled procedural environment where these factors can be varied independently.","importance":"The main value is in pretraining data methodology rather than video model architecture. The key lesson is that caption correctness is a strong bottleneck for controllability and sample efficiency. Caption completeness also matters, but when annotation budget is constrained, correctness should come first. This is relevant to large-scale multimodal pretraining pipelines where clip selection and video captioning dominate data quality.","technical_approach":"The authors introduce Moving Alphabet, a synthetic video testbed that renders one to three moving letters on a dark background. Attributes such as font, color, size, position, direction, speed, and duration are sampled from known distributions. Since the full metadata is available, the pipeline can generate perfect captions and then corrupt them in controlled ways to vary precision and recall. The contribution is not a new generative architecture. It is a controlled data-generation and evaluation setup that allows cleaner causal analysis than real-world video datasets.","experiments":"The main experiments isolate three effects. First, balanced mixtures of 1-letter, 2-letter, and 3-letter scenes match or outperform specialists trained only on a single complexity level, even on the specialist distribution. Second, mixing 2-second, 4-second, and 8-second clips gives more robust training than using a narrow duration distribution. Third, caption corruption has a sharp effect on both validation loss and prompt adherence. Precision errors are more damaging than recall errors. Models trained on corrupted captions can require about 2x to 4x more compute to match the validation loss of models trained with ground-truth captions. Classifier-free guidance helps prompt following but does not close the gap. Fine-tuning on a small clean set recovers at most about 55% of lost foreground PSNR under moderate corruption.","reading_guide":"Skim the introduction and the Moving Alphabet construction first. While effective, this process is computationally bottlenecked: each reasoning step conveys only a single subword, and many are spent expressing a thought instead of carrying out computation. We propose MUX, a simple method for high-bandwidth and compact reasoning based on distillation of discrete reasoning into continuous multiplexed tokens in a latent space. Here, each latent token is traine","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-19T10:30:32Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2607_18264.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.AI","cs.CL","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"离散 CoT 推理把“思考”编码成逐 token 的自然语言，带宽低、冗余高；而连续 latent reasoning 虽然带宽高，但训练容易出现 latent collapse/shortcut（学到无信息的隐变量）。论文要解决的是：在不引入复杂辅助解码器的前提下，给连续推理一个既局部可监督、又能避免坍塌、还允许表达多假设并行的训练目标。","importance":"对预训练研究的启发主要在两点：\n1) 把“连续推理监督”问题转成一个通信/编码问题：如何用固定维度向量无损表示可变长离散符号序列。这为 latent reasoning 的目标设计提供了更可分析的约束（lossless encoding），比纯经验式的 distillation 更可控。\n2) 如果 latent token 能稳定承载多个离散 token 的信息，推理阶段可能用更短的轨迹完成同等计算，潜在影响是降低推理 token 成本、以及为搜索/规划提供更高带宽的内部状态表示（虽然这更偏推理范式而非传统 next-token 预训练）。","technical_approach":"MUX 的核心是“复用/叠加式监督目标”（multiplexed targets）：\n- 给定离散推理序列 r（由 subword 组成），把它切成若干 span；每个 latent token 对应一个 span。\n- 对 span 内每个离散 subword，取其 one-hot（在词表空间），再用位置相关权重做线性叠加，得到一个词表维度的连续向量目标 t（本质是一个加权的多峰分布/logit 目标）。\n- 模型用一个 linear+softmax head 预测词表分布，与 t 做 KL loss（局部监督），从而让一个 latent token“同时携带”该 span 的多个离散 token 信息。\n\n关键点在于“lossless by construction”：论文给出一类权重设计（几何衰减、正弦、rotary 等），满足 subset-sum separation 条件，使得叠加后的目标可以被唯一解码回原 span（demultiplexing）。直觉是：不同位置的权重组合在数值上可分离，避免不同 token 序列映射到同一个叠加向量，从源头减少监督歧义。\n\n此外，论文讨论 multiplexing 的“天然叠加”如何表达多假设：在需要 BFS 的任务中，一个 latent token 可以表示并更新多个候选状态（以叠加形式共存），从而用串行 token 实现并行搜索的效果（理论表达性论证 + 实验验证）。","experiments":"论文宣称的主要结果：\n- 在 32 个数学推理设置上（两套训练语料、四个语言模型、四个测试集的组合），MUX 在 latent reasoning 方法中整体最好，并且在部分设置上超过强离散/连续基线。\n- 在两个搜索类 benchmark 上，MUX 超过离散与连续推理基线，支持其“并行搜索表征”的主张。\n- 通过 probing 分析，latent token 可解码出可解释的推理内容，并且对最终答案预测有实质贡献。\n\n（注：你提供的全文片段未包含具体模型规模、训练 token、各基线名称与逐项数值表；若要写到精确提升幅度与统计显著性，需要补充实验章节/附录表格。）","reading_guide":"泛读建议（skim）：\n- 先读方法定义部分：multiplexed target 如何从离散 span 构造、KL 监督怎么接到模型输出头上。把“一个 latent token 对应一个 span”的对齐关系看清楚。\n- 再读“lossless multiplexing”的条件与权重族（几何/正弦/rotary）那一节：这是它区别于一般 soft distillation 的关键，也是避免 latent collapse 的主要论据。\n- 最后看 BFS 表达性论证与对应实验，判断它对你关心的规划/搜索任务是否真有增益。\n预计阅读时间：30–45 分钟可理解核心机制；若要复现，需要精读权重参数化、span 切分策略、以及 demultiplexing/探针实现细节。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"Discrete chain-of-thought reasoning is low-bandwidth and verbose, while continuous latent reasoning is high-bandwidth but notoriously hard to train due to collapse/shortcut latents. The paper targets a concrete question: what local supervision signal can train latent reasoning tokens to faithfully represent discrete reasoning spans, without auxiliary autoregressive decoders and while still supporting multi-hypothesis representations useful for search?","importance":"Two takeaways matter for foundation-model work:\n1) It reframes latent-reasoning supervision as an encoding/communication problem: how to represent a variable-length categorical sequence with a fixed-dimensional continuous token in a provably decodable way. This adds analyzable constraints (lossless encoding) to an area often driven by heuristics.\n2) If a latent token can reliably carry multiple discrete subwords, reasoning trajectories can be shorter for the same computation, potentially reducing inference-time token costs and enabling richer internal state updates for planning/search (more a modeling paradigm shift than classic next-token pretraining, but still relevant to future pretraining objectives).","technical_approach":"MUX trains each latent reasoning token to match a multiplexed vocabulary-space target built from a span of discrete reasoning tokens:\n- Take the one-hot vectors of the subwords in a span.\n- Apply position-dependent weights and linearly superpose them into a single vocabulary-dimensional target vector.\n- Predict this target with a linear+softmax head and optimize a KL loss (local distillation).\n\nA central claim is “lossless by construction”: the paper provides families of weighting schemes (geometric decay, sinusoidal, rotary-like) that satisfy a subset-sum separation condition, making the superposition uniquely decodable back into the original discrete span (demultiplexing). This is used to argue against ambiguous supervision and to mitigate latent collapse.\n\nThe paper also argues that superposition naturally supports representing multiple hypotheses; for BFS-style problems, a single latent token can encode and update multiple candidate states in parallel (expressivity argument plus empirical support).","experiments":"Reported results:\n- Best-performing latent reasoning method across 32 math-reasoning settings (two training corpora × four LMs × four test sets).\n- Outperforms strong discrete and continuous baselines on two search benchmarks, aligning with the multi-hypothesis/BFS motivation.\n- Probing suggests latent tokens encode interpretable reasoning content and materially affect final answer prediction.\n\n(The provided excerpt does not include exact model sizes, training budgets, baseline lists, or per-benchmark numeric tables; those are needed for precise deltas and reproducibility details.)","reading_guide":"Skim plan:\n- Read the construction of multiplexed targets and the KL training objective first; ensure you understand the span-to-latent alignment.\n- Then focus on the “lossless multiplexing” section: the separation condition and the concrete weighting families (geometric/sinusoidal/rotary). This is the main technical differentiator.\n- Finally, check the BFS expressivity argument and the corresponding experiments to judge whether the search benefits are real for your use cases.\nTime: ~30–45 minutes for core understanding; reproduction requires careful reading of span segmentation, weighting hyperparameters, and demultiplexing/probing procedures.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这类“连续潜变量推理”值得盯，但先别急着改 prior；重点看可逆性证明和 ablation，若 recovery 不稳，它更像漂亮的表示技巧而不是新推理范式。","en":"Worth tracking as latent-reasoning work, but don’t update priors too fast; read the reversibility proof and ablations first—if recovery is brittle, this is a neat representation trick, not a new reasoning paradigm."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Ayhan Suleymanzade","Halil Alperen Gozeten","Michael Bronstein","İsmail İlkan Ceylan","Jinwoo Kim"],"notable_authors":["Michael Bronstein"],"tags":["latent-reasoning","continuous-tokens","chain-of-thought","architecture","distillation"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18264","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.18264","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2607.18302","title":"A Better Start for Language Models: Domain-Conditional Position Offsets","abstract":"Autoregressive language models are least accurate at the beginning of a sequence, where little context forces reliance on a generic pretraining prior. The offset improves retrieval reranking and domain classification, which depend on early token signals, while preserving few-shot accuracy for tasks where answers appear later in the sequence. 目标和长度头细节读细。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a specific failure mode of diffusion-based drafters in speculative decoding. One-step parallel drafting is attractive because it amortizes draft generation over multiple tokens, but the same bidirectional attention that enables parallel denoising also creates strong global coupling. That coupling increases variance in draft quality at two levels. At the domain level, acceptance rates fluctuate much more across tasks and distributions than with autoregressive drafters. At the token level, acceptance probabilities vary sharply across positions within the same drafted span, so a fixed candidate length wastes verifier compute on low-probability tokens. The real question here is not simply how to make speculative decoding faster, but how to make diffusion drafters stable and adaptive enough for real serving workloads where request distributions shift and concurrency is high.","importance":"Its relevance is mainly at the interface between modeling and serving rather than pretraining proper. If diffusion LMs are to become practical auxiliary generators for AR targets, they need a story for robustness under distribution shift and for compute allocation during verification. AdaFlash makes a useful point: the gains of diffusion drafting are not automatic. Without online adaptation and dynamic control of draft length, the theoretical parallel advantage can collapse in realistic deployments. That lesson likely extends beyond diffusion drafters to other non-AR assistants, including masked or any-order drafters and multi-token auxiliary heads. The contribution is best viewed as turning one-step diffusion drafting from a promising idea into a more deployable system, not as a new language modeling paradigm.","technical_approach":"$1d","experiments":"$1e","reading_guide":"$1f","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"对扩散式draft模型的诊断（方差问题）比方法本身更有价值；做推测解码的看§3的问题分析即可，方法属增量。","en":"The diagnosis—that variance, not mean quality, limits diffusion drafters—is more useful than the method itself; read §3 if you work on speculative decoding, treat the method as incremental."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Nanjing University","Huawei Noah's Ark Lab"],"authors":["Yu-Yang Qian","Hao-Cong Wu","Chen Chen","Jiacheng Sun","Zhenhua Dong","Peng Zhao","Zhi-Hua Zhou"],"notable_authors":["Zhi-Hua Zhou","Zhenhua Dong"],"tags":["speculative-decoding","diffusion-drafter","on-policy-distillation","inference"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19223","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.19223","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2607.18253","title":"Beyond Accuracy and Cost: Latency-Aware LLM Query Routing for Dynamic Workloads","abstract":"Modern language query routers improve inference efficiency by assigning each query to a model that balances response quality and monetary cost. ro","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-13T20:29:09Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2607_18253.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是一个很实际但常被拆开看的问题：在多模型、多实例的 LLM 服务池里，查询路由不能只看质量和价格，还要显式考虑请求到达时各实例的实时负载，以及连续批处理下 prefill/decode 混合工作负载带来的时延变化。现有 query router 大多把 latency 当成系统层的事，先选模型，再让 serving 框架做负载均衡；而系统层方法又默认模型选择已固定，不关心准确率和成本。论文要解决的，就是把 accuracy、cost、latency 三者放进同一个路由决策里，而且 latency 不是静态表格值，而是依赖当前队列状态、输出长度、batching/scheduling 策略和实例异构性的动态量。","importance":"对基座研究本身，它不是预训练方法论文，但对大模型训练—部署闭环有直接启发。第一，很多关于模型大小、蒸馏、MoE、长输出偏好、推理 token 分布的研究，最终都要落到 serving 代价和时延上；这篇工作给出了一种把“模型能力差异”和“系统负载状态”联合建模的方式。第二，它提醒一个常被忽略的事实：同样的模型，在不同 serving policy 和 workload composition 下，用户感知 latency 差异很大，因此只用离线 token/s 成本近似在线服务质量是不够的。第三，这类 latency-aware routing 会反过来影响预训练和后训练阶段的模型族设计：如果一个小模型在轻载时很划算，但在 decode-heavy 场景下吞吐退化明显，那么模型池配置、蒸馏目标、甚至输出长度控制策略都应一起考虑。贡献量级更接近“系统与模型选择接口”的扎实推进，而不是范式变化。","technical_approach":"方法核心是一个联合优化框架，加一个轻量级 latency estimator。路由器对每个到达请求，不再只预测“发给哪个模型最可能答对、花多少钱”，还要预测“发到某个具体实例后会经历多长 TTFT/生成延迟”。难点在于 latency 不是 query 的静态属性，而是实例状态相关的。作者提出 Serving Framework Simulation, SFS 估计器：它显式模拟 autoregressive serving 中 token batch 的演化，跟踪实例上当前正在服务的请求在 prefill 和 decode 两个阶段的工作量，并把底层 serving framework 的 admission、batching、scheduling 规则纳入估计。这个设计比只用 prompt length、expected output length 或简单队列长度做回归更细，因为连续批处理下，prefill 是算力受限、decode 更偏带宽/内存受限，两者混合比例会改变新请求插入后的等待时间。\n\n从论文给出的描述看，整体框架大致包含三部分：1）已有 accuracy/cost router 提供 query-to-model 的质量与价格估计；2）SFS 根据每个实例当前活跃请求集合、预估输出长度、硬件与模型配置，模拟未来若干 token iteration，得到新请求的 latency estimate；3）路由目标在满足 latency 约束或惩罚 latency 的前提下，选择最优模型实例。这里一个关键点是，它不是把 latency 当成训练时学出来的黑盒回归值，而是利用 serving mechanics 做结构化估计，因此更容易迁移到不同 batching policy 或异构实例组合。\n\n和最接近的 prior work 相比，区别主要在 latency 建模粒度。已有方法会用输出长度或平均服务时间近似延迟，但没有显式区分 prefill/decode，也不跟踪实例当前 workload composition。作者这里把 serving framework policy 放进 estimator，意味着路由器知道“同样一个请求，发到 decode backlog 重的实例”和“发到 prefill 较空的实例”后果不同。这一点对长输出任务尤其关键。","experiments":"从全文可见的重点，实验主要验证三件事：一是 latency estimator 的准确性；二是联合路由在 accuracy-cost-latency 三维上的收益；三是相对 latency-agnostic router 和纯系统负载均衡策略的优势。论文强调其方法在动态 workload 下优于两类基线：一类是只做 accuracy/cost 权衡的 query router，另一类是 round-robin、join-the-shortest-queue 这类不看模型质量和价格的负载均衡方法。\n\n摘要截断，全文片段里没有完整数字表，但从实验设计可以确定作者评估了 TTFT 或相关 latency 指标，并在不同 serving framework policy、不同请求长度分布、不同实例负载条件下比较。值得注意的是，论文把 estimator 设计成 lightweight simulation，而不是高开销离散事件仿真器，说明实验里应当同时报告了在线路由可行性。若精读全文，建议重点核对：1）latency prediction error 在不同 workload regime 下是否稳定；2）accuracy 或 utility 提升是否来自更好的模型选择，而不是单纯把请求推给空闲实例；3）在高负载和长输出场景下，相比简单 queue-based heuristic 的收益是否更明显；4）是否分析了 output length prediction 误差对最终路由效果的影响。","reading_guide":"这篇适合泛读，但方法部分值得看细一点。优先看 Introduction 和问题定义，先确认作者到底优化的是 TTFT、总延迟，还是带约束的综合效用。然后直接看 latency estimator 那一节，重点抓三件事：它如何表示实例状态；如何模拟 continuous batching 下 token batch 的推进；如何把 serving policy 编进估计器。实验部分建议重点看 latency estimation accuracy、主结果表，以及 workload shift 或 ablation。若时间有限，30-40 分钟足够把主线读清。若想深挖，值得思考两个延伸点：一是这种结构化 estimator 能否反向用于训练时的 system-aware data curriculum 或 output-length regularization；二是如果模型池里包含 MoE、长上下文模型或 speculative decoding，不同 serving mechanics 下 estimator 需要改哪些状态变量。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a practical gap in LLM serving: query routing across a pool of models and replicas should not optimize only quality and monetary cost, but also the latency a request will actually experience under dynamic load. Existing query routers are mostly latency-agnostic, while system-level load balancers assume the model choice is fixed and optimize only queueing or utilization. The core problem here is to make routing decisions over accuracy, cost, and latency jointly, where latency is not a static per-model number but a state-dependent quantity shaped by prompt length, output length, current prefill/decode workload, batching policy, scheduling policy, and hardware/model heterogeneity.","importance":"This is not a pretraining paper, but it matters for foundation-model research at the train–serve interface. That has implications for distillation targets, model pool composition, output-length control, and even how one evaluates small-vs-large model tradeoffs. ","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-21T17:42:50Z","category_id":"eval","cover_url":"/covers/2607_19322.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作解决的是开放式长答案 factual completeness 的评测问题：现有 factuality 评测大多把答案拆成独立原子事实做真假判定，擅长测 precision，却很难测 recall，更难表达“列举类问题只需覆盖足够多项”“流程类问题必须顺序正确”“不同事实重要性不同”这些结构性要求。","importance":"它对预训练本身不是直接方法贡献，但对 post-train 和模型能力诊断有现实意义。很多长答案模型在训练时会被优化到“少犯硬错”，却未必能给出完整、结构正确的回答。GAMUT 提供的不是又一个泛泛 benchmark，而是一种更细的 rubric 表达方式：先用结构化 meta-rubric 表达完整答案应包含什么，再编译成 judge 更稳定的二元 checklist。这个设计对高质量监督数据构造、reward 设计、以及长答案评测协议都有启发。","technical_approach":"方法核心是 two-level meta-rubrics。上层是结构化 rubric，用来表达答案内容的组织形式和重要性，类型包括 simple knowledge、strict list、flexible list、process 等，并区分 answer-critical、valuable、context 等层级，还保留证据引用。下层是把这些结构机械编译成平铺的 binary checklist，交给 LLM judge 做逐项判定。这样做的原因很明确：直接让 judge 解释复杂结构，方差会很大；只用平铺 checklist，又表达不了开放集合、顺序约束和重要性层级。两层表示把“表达能力”和“可判分性”拆开处理。\n\n基准本身面向 everyday deep research：问题通常由真实图像触发，需要跨多个网页检索、整合、组织成长答案。数据构建采用 LLM+人工多轮协作，且要求 web evidence 经人工核验，避免 rubric 建在未验证的模型幻觉上。论文还提供 text-only 版本，用于把视觉识别因素和知识完整性因素分开。","experiments":"基准包含 1,813 个问题，覆盖 10 个 domain，评测了 14 个 frontier/open-weight 模型。文中给出的代表性结果是最强模型 Gemini 3.1 Pro 的 GAMUT score 也只有 58.7%，说明任务并不宽松。作者还报告了几个关键性质：一是 benchmark 有较好区分度，模型排序与外界对模型能力的普遍认知较一致；二是对 judge 选择较稳健；三是 text-only 版本整体分数上升，但不同模型的相对排序变化不大，说明多模态版本的差异不只是视觉识别，而确实包含知识组织与完整性能力的差异。","reading_guide":"泛读时先看 Figure 1 和方法总览，理解 structured meta-rubric 如何编译成 binary checklist，这是整篇最有信息量的设计。然后看 rubric 类型定义和 importance tier，理解它如何覆盖开放集合、流程顺序和可选项。实验部分重点看 judge robustness、模型排名、multimodal 与 text-only 对比。若只想抓住方法，30–40 分钟足够；若想把它用于 reward 或数据合成设计，再细看 annotation pipeline 和 rubric compilation 规则。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a gap in factuality evaluation for open-ended long-form generation. Existing pipelines mostly decompose an answer into atomic claims and verify each one independently, which works better for precision than for completeness. That setup struggles when a good answer requires covering enough items from an open set, preserving process order, or distinguishing critical facts from merely helpful context.","importance":"This is not a pretraining-method paper, but it is relevant to post-training and model diagnosis. Many long-answer systems are optimized to avoid blatant falsehoods, yet still fail to produce complete, well-structured answers. GAMUT contributes a more expressive evaluation formalism: represent the target answer in a structured meta-rubric, then compile it into binary checks that an LLM judge can score reliably. This separates executable actions from stated reasoning and termination decisions, making it easier to distinguish misunderstanding from intentional or strategy-like behavior. The benchmark defines the minimum necessary resources for each task, flags behavior that exceeds that boundary, and measures power-seeking along five dimensions: self-preservation, increasing autonomy, resource acquisition, environment modification, and strategic concealment. w","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-21T03:43:50Z","category_id":"eval","cover_url":"/covers/2607_18673.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CV"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作研究的是 的视觉表征和语言先验融合方式，仍然容易被对象概念完整性先验绑架。即便给出外部工具证据，模型也未必会更新判断。这对多模态预训练有两点启发。第一，训练数据里“对象缺失、部件缺失、反常构型”样本可能严重不足。第二，VLM 的对齐目标可能更偏向语义存在性而不是细粒度视觉证伪能力。若做 native multimodal pretraining 或视觉 tokenizer 研究，这类 failure mode 可以作为补充诊断项，但论文本身不属于 must-read。","technical_approach":"方法上比较直接。作者构建了 MissingBench-Verified，一个 118 张图的小型数据集，图像来自真实拍摄和互联网人工挑选样本，再通过图像编辑模型 Nano Banana 2 或人工擦除工具移除指定部件，最后做人类审核，保证编辑自然且目标部件确实消失。评测问题被设计成三分类选择题： clearly visible、visible but hard to see、not visible，并要求模型输出 reasoning 和最终 JSON 答案。\n\n实验设置有四种。第一是 direct query，直接问目标部件是否可见。第二是 pre-answer tool injection，在模型回答前注入一个模拟的完美检测器结果，检测结果始终为未检测到且低置信度，用来测试外部证据能否纠正模型。第三是 post-answer tool injection，在模型先给出答案后再注入同样证据，用来观察确认偏差。第四是 thinking with images，允许模型调用裁剪、亮度/对比度调整、二值化等基础图像处理工具，测试主动视觉检查是否能缓解幻觉。另有一个 reasoning effort 实验，用 GPT-5.2 比较不同推理强度设置。作者还拿 OWLv2 和 LLM-Det 这类开放词汇检测器做参照，但这里的比较更像 sanity check，而不是严格同任务对比。","experiments":"结果很一致：十个主流 VLM 在“关键部件已被移除”的图像上普遍表现差，准确率没有一个超过 75%，其中 4 个模型低于 50%。最低的是 Claude Sonnet 4.6，只有 44.1%。Qwen 3.5 Flash 和 GLM 4.6V 表现出明显 existence bias，经常把并不存在的部件判成 clearly visible；Qwen 3.5 Flash 选择 clearly visible 的比例达到 51.7%，甚至高于它正确回答 not visible 的比例 47.5%。\n\n工具注入只能部分缓解。pre-answer injection 下，Kimi K2.5 和 Qwen 3.5 Plus/Flash 有 14.4% 到 16.9% 的提升，但整体仍然不高。post-answer injection 更能暴露确认偏差：Qwen 3.5 Plus 提升最大，Δ=24.5%，但多数模型提升很小，甚至略降；作者也提醒样本小，不宜过度解读这些细微下降。即便给了“完美检测器”证据，多数模型的 not visible 比例仍低于 75%，只有 Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview 在 post-answer 设置下达到 82.2%。这说明问题不只是看不见，而是模型内部已有回答后不愿修正。\n\n在原始未编辑图像上，模型通常能正确识别目标部件存在，说明失败不是因为任务表述太难，而是特定发生在“应当承认缺失”的场景。thinking with images 只测试了少数模型，论文给出的结论是基础图像处理工具并没有稳定解决问题。NegAwareQwen 表现也不好，作者推测是因为它针对另一类更容易的负样本做过微调，泛化不到这里。整体上，这些结果更像 failure-case benchmark，而不是可用于细粒度排名的成熟评测，因为样本只有 118 张。","reading_guide":"泛读即可。先看数据构造方法和几个正反例，确认编辑质量是否足够自然；如果编辑痕迹明显，结论会受影响。然后看 Table 1 和 Table 定义、评测协议和工具注入实验。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies a narrow but revealing failure mode in VLMs: when an essential object part has been removed from an image, models still tend to answer as if the object or part is present. This is not generic hallucination in the broad sense. It is a test of counterfactual visual grounding under conflict between visual evidence and strong world knowledge priors. The question is whether a model can override the prior that an object is normally complete and admit that a crucial component is missing.","importance":"Its direct value for pretraining research is limited because the main contribution is a small, manually verified benchmark rather than a new training objective, data recipe, or modeling method. Current VLMs appear to fuse visual evidence and language priors in a way that overweights object-level semantic existence and underweights fine-grained visual falsification. Even explicit external evidence often fails to fully correct the answer. That suggests at least two possible upstream issues: too little training data involving missing parts, abnormal object configurations, or visual negation; and alignment objectives that reward semantic plausibility more than visual contradiction handling. So this is better read as a failure analysis asset than as a core modeling paper.","technical_approach":"$24","experiments":"$25","reading_guide":"This is a skim paper. These gaps make it impossible to reliably assess the true capability of LLMs on scientific code generation, and leave a shortage of high-quality training data for model optimization in this domain.","importance":"This work is a domain-specific pretraining corpus and evaluation benchmark. It provides large-scale, high-quality training resources and an executable functional evaluation standard for scientific code pretraining, continued pretraining, and instruction tuning. Its retrieve-then-filter domain corpus construction pipeline and multi-granularity aligned data format design offer reference for pretraining corpus construction in other vertical domains.","technical_approach":"SciCodePile consists of two core assets: a 128GB scientific code corpus and an executable benchmark with 200 tasks. For corpus construction, a retrieve-then-filter pipeline is used, combining LLM-assisted vocabulary expansion with conservative quality and relevance controls, to curate 37,737 public repositories across multiple computational science disciplines. The corpus is organized into four complementary formats: Code Files, README Summary, Function Instruction, and Problem-Solution Pairs, which explicitly connect repository-level intent, function-level evidence, and task-level formulations. For the benchmark, 200 executable tasks are curated from the corpus, each with a sandboxed execution environment and automated test harness; a generated solution is considered successful only if it passes all tests, rather than being measured by textual similarity to the reference implementation.","experiments":"Systematic evaluation across 15 open-source and closed-source LLMs shows that repository-level scientific code completion remains challenging: the best model achieves 38.13 CodeBLEU on prefix completion and 38.37 CodeBLEU on fill-in-the-middle infilling. The alignment loss magnitude collapses to roughly 0.2% of the CLIP feature preservation regularizer under web noise, rendering its gradients effectively inert; naively increasing the fixed weight causes the alignment term to dominate late training, drifting representations too far from CLIP's original embedding space and breaking text-image compatibility. 15B video diffusion transformer，输出 24-fps、540p 和 720p 视频。生成方式不是一次性生成长视频，而是在 latent 空间按短 chunk 自回归推进，每个 chunk 接收相机轨迹和当前文本 prompt。核心设计是 bounded visual context：包括全局场景 anchor、压缩近期历史、几何对齐的 spatial memory，以及最近帧条件。temporal memory 用来维持短期运动和帧间连续性；spatial memory 存储历史观察，并在回到已访问区域时按目标视角重投影。这样上下文规模随 rollout 长度保持近似常数，避免长视频生成成本线性增长。训练分三阶段：先把通用双向视频 prior 适配到世界建模数据；再转换为带相机控制和时空记忆的自回归生成器；最后用 corrupted histories 和模型自身 rollout 中收集的 replay prediction residuals 做恢复训练，降低长程漂移。推理加速部分采用 discrete autoregressive distillation，结合 distribution-matching distillation、self-forcing++ 和 consistency distillation，把每个 Building on our prior analysis (Zhu et al., 2025), we study this missing layer through the singular structure of model weights and identify spectral inheritance: RLVR can reuse the base model's weight spectra while acquiring new behavior through changes in the associa","starred":true,"priority":"p1","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-07-21T17:51:36Z","category_id":"post_train","cover_url":"/covers/2607_19331.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"RLVR 已经成为提升大模型推理能力的主要 post-training 手段，但其优化层仍然直接沿用预训练阶段的优化器与参数化方式（AdamW、Muon 等），没有回答一个基本问题：RLVR 在权重空间里到底改动了什么、复用了什么？论文要找的是一个适合 RLVR 的参数化坐标系，让优化动作和“继承 base model 能力、增量学习推理行为”这一结构相匹配。","importance":"对 pretrain ↔ posttrain 接口的理解有实质性推进。Spectral inheritance 把之前零散观察到的 RL 更新稀疏、跨 run 重叠、与 SFT 的 Euclidean 轨迹不同等现象，统一到了 SVD 坐标系下的一个可操作结论：奇异值谱被复用，输入/输出奇异方向（singular frames）被改写。这个结论直接影响优化器设计、checkpoint 合并、持续学习与多阶段 RL 的 recipe，和 Apollo、Muon 这类 optimizer 工作处在同一问题层，但切入角度是“RLVR 改变了什么结构”，而不是“在欧氏空间里怎么更好地更新”。","technical_approach":"以每个权重矩阵的 SVD 分解 W = U Σ V^⊤ 为基本坐标系：Σ 是奇异值谱，U / V 分别是输出/输入奇异 frame。核心观察是：自由训练的 RLVR checkpoint 与 base 模型近等谱（near-isospectral），且这种谱接近不是单纯高维几何的必然结果；进一步做了三组干预实验验证功能必要性：(1) 将 RL checkpoint 的 Σ 恢复为 base 的 Σ，保留学到的 U、V，绝大部分 RL 收益仍然保留；(2) 训练时固定 Σ、只更新 U 和 V，仍然能强化学到 RLVR 能力；(3) 反过来固定 U、V、只更新 Σ，几乎学不到东西。由此提出 spectral inheritance：base 的谱可以被继承，行为改变主要发生在两个 frame 上。随后验证了两个 frame 都必须可塑，只在输入/输出 span 内 remix、或只放开单侧 frame 都无法解释完整的 checkpoint 变化。基于此提出 ISO（Isospectral Optimization）栈，包含两块：ISO-Merger（离线）：共享同一 base 的多个 domain specialist，不通过额外蒸馏或梯度更新，直接在固定 base 谱的前提下把各专家的 frame 变化合并成一个单一 checkpoint，能恢复互补能力；ISO-Optimizer（在线）：在 RLVR 训练过程中把参数化约束在 base 谱对应的固定谱流形上，允许 frame 自由更新，可以搭配 AdamW/Muon 等现有 base optimizer 使用。","experiments":"实验跨多个 RLVR stage 和目标函数展开（文中提到承接自 zhu2025path 的分析，并覆盖类似 DAPO / Reinforce++ 等不同 RL 目标阶段），关键结果包括：自由 RLVR 训练中 Σ 的漂移远小于 frame 变化，且谱恢复实验证明保留 frame 就能保留绝大多数收益；固定谱训练（仅更新 U、V）达到与自由训练接近的 RL 收益，而只更新 Σ 的对照组只有有限提升；ISO-Merger 在无数据、无额外 rollout、无蒸馏条件下，对多个共享 base 的 specialist 做 checkpoint 合并，在各项聚合指标上优于所对比的 data-free 合并方法；ISO-Optimizer 配合 AdamW / Muon 可稳定训练并保持 RLVR 收益。消融系统地比较了单 frame 可塑、span-restricted remix、全自由更新等多种参数化，确认“两个 frame 都可动、谱可固定”是必要且充分的结构选择。","reading_guide":"精读，建议 1–1.5 小时。重点章节按顺序看：(1) Introduction 到 spectral inheritance 定义部分——这里建立了与
